# Ostrog reference

New here? HOW-WE-GOT-HERE (not included in this source snapshot) maps where the plan, the decisions,
each checkpoint's review and the reason for every fix are kept.

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

Ostrog is an autonomous colony simulation meant to run on a second monitor. Ten people
come through a portal into a valley; three of them land together and the other seven are
scattered, alive or dead, and nobody knows which. Each of them arrives with a life already
lived (parents, losses, a trade, things they came to believe), and from there they forage,
build, farm, mine, hunt, cook, quarrel, pair, raise children, form customs and send parties
past the edge of the map. You watch; you do not command. Every line of history the game shows
comes from a recorded event, and the simulation never calls a language model.

The simulation is deterministic Rust (`crates/`), the client is Godot 4.6.3 with GDScript
(`godot/`), and every sprite and sound is generated by Python scripts in `tools/art` and
`tools/audio`. The frames above are from unscripted seeded runs (see
docs/reviews/render-2026-10-16 (not included in this source snapshot) and
docs/reviews/techniques-2026-10-16 (not included in this source snapshot)).

For current project direction, read `VISION.md`, `DECISIONS.md` D59–D64,
`docs/design-minds-observation.md`, `docs/ART_BIBLE.md`, `docs/ART_SPEC.md` and
`DEFINITION_OF_DONE.md`. Active development and generated output stay on D:; C: and P: paths in
older checkpoint notes are historical. Art mockups are reference material; production art requires
real runtime assets, provenance, guards and integrated-game evidence.

This page is the tester's reference: how to build and run it, every word and sentence the
game can show, every asset file, the simulation facts that decide whether something is a bug,
and the roadmap. The long tables are generated from the code (see
[Regenerating the tables](#regenerating-the-tables)).

## 1. What you see

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

Seed 1, `--opening=steady --ticks=2400 --inspect=1`, two game-hours after the arrival. Top
left is the HUD (valley name, calendar, view level, population, community, speed, director,
the deity who watches). Bottom left is the feed. On the right is the inspect panel for Aelsin: activity,
community and role, needs, traits, skills, mood, people, beliefs with their sources, and the
life before the portal.

A fresh hands-on launch opens on the new-game wizard, six pages: who watches (the deity, Aun,
Hask or Mirre, who paces the world), the valley (a seed, three character sliders or "Shape the
valley myself", how deep the valley's past runs, how long the world has stood, and where on
the world map the valley lies, with a picture of the valley that place makes, drawn in the
game's own ground colours), the peoples (how many, which kinds and how rare, and what this
seed would draw), who came through (ten cards, each with a name, age, traits, three best skills
and three lines of the life they lived; choose three), what they hold (the beliefs they share),
and Begin. The three step through at 07:00 on day 1 of spring,
Year 1. The first day is foraging and the first walls; over a few game-days a house with beds,
a hearth, a field, a stockpile and a quarry shaft appear. Strangers turn up at the valley edge
and are taken in or turned away; some bring word of the missing, and someone may set out to
look. Fires, floods, cold, wounds, fever, quarrels and breakdowns happen and are met by
whoever is there, with whatever they know. Births take years. Customs grow from things that
happened and can be traced in the History panel.

## 2. Build, run, test

### Requirements

| What | Version | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Rust | 1.95.0 | pinned in `rust-toolchain.toml` |
| Godot | 4.6.3 | `tools/godot` finds `.tools/godot4`, `.tools/godot4.exe`, a sibling checkout's copy, then `godot4` or `godot` on PATH |
| Godot export templates | 4.6.3 | only for `tools/package.sh` |
| cargo-xwin | any | only to build the Windows DLL on Linux |
| Python 3.11+ | with Pillow and numpy | art and audio generators; `tools/docs/readme_images.py` |
| ffmpeg with libvorbis | | `tools/audio/sfx.py` writes OGG through it |
| Xvfb | | Linux screenshots: `tools/shots/shot.sh` draws on display `:96` |

### Play a release package

**Quick start, Windows (x64).** Nothing needs installing first.

1. Open the download page from the link you were sent and press **Download Ostrog
   (Windows)**. You get `ostrog-windows-x64-portable-<build>.zip`.
2. Right-click it, **Extract All**, to a folder you own (Documents or `D:\Games`, not Program
   Files). Do not run it from inside the zip window.
3. Open the `ostrog-windows-x64-portable` folder and double-click `ostrog-launcher.exe`.
   A small window says what it is doing ("Checking for a newer version...", then
   "Downloading Ostrog <version>: ..." when there is an update, or "Can't reach the update server. Starting your
   installed version." when you are offline, and "Starting the game. A window will open
   shortly."), then the game opens on the new-game wizard.
4. To update, just start the launcher again: it checks once per start and installs a newer
   version only if its signature and checksum verify. To uninstall, delete the folder.

Your saves and settings are not in that folder: they are in
`%APPDATA%\Godot\app_userdata\Ostrog\` (see [Saves](#saves) below), so updates
and uninstalling leave them alone. The folder's `data\` holds only the launcher's log
(`data\logs\launcher.log`). Keep the launcher where it is, outside the replaceable `app\`
folder; it never contains a GitHub credential. docs/release-updater.md (not included in this source snapshot)
has the install layout and publication contract, and
[docs/privacy/FILES-ON-YOUR-COMPUTER.md](privacy/FILES-ON-YOUR-COMPUTER.md) every file the
game creates. (`ostrog-windows-x64.zip`, without "portable", is the app-only archive the
launcher downloads for an update; `app\ostrog.exe` starts the game without the update check.)

Linux (x86_64), developer package (not published to friends):

    tar xf ostrog-linux-x86_64.tar.gz
    cd ostrog-linux-x86_64
    ./ostrog.x86_64

Client flags go after a `--` separator, for example
`./ostrog.x86_64 -- --seed=7` or `app\ostrog.exe -- --opening=relentless`. Through the
launcher they need a second one, because the launcher passes on what follows its own `--`:
`ostrog-launcher.exe -- -- --opening=relentless`. See
[Client command-line flags](#client-command-line-flags).

### Build from source

Simulation and CLI:

    cargo build --release -p hf-cli          # target/release/ostrog
    cargo run --release -p hf-cli -- --help

The Godot extension (the client cannot start without it; `godot/bin/` is not in git):

    # Linux
    cargo build --release -p hf-godot
    mkdir -p godot/bin/linux
    cp target/release/libhf_godot.so godot/bin/linux/

    # Windows, built natively
    cargo build --release -p hf-godot
    mkdir -p godot/bin/windows
    cp target/release/hf_godot.dll godot/bin/windows/

    # Windows DLL cross-built on Linux
    cargo xwin build --release -p hf-godot --target x86_64-pc-windows-msvc
    cp target/x86_64-pc-windows-msvc/release/hf_godot.dll godot/bin/windows/

To skip the Windows build, copy the box's prebuilt DLL for your commit. After every master
checkpoint the box publishes it to the mirror at
`<mirror>\prebuilt\windows\<full commit hash>\hf_godot.dll` (with a `.sha256`
next to it), and `prebuilt\windows\latest.txt` names the newest one (`commit`, `date`, `tag`,
`built`, `sha256`, `path` lines). Use the DLL of the commit you have checked out. Details:
tools/prebuilt/README.md (not included in this source snapshot).

For a dedicated Windows watch-copy, create a desktop shortcut to `watch-copy.cmd` in the
repository root. Opening it fast-forward updates a clean `master`, installs the checksum-verified
prebuilt DLL for that exact commit (or builds it locally if publication has not caught up), runs a
Godot import so the new extension and assets are registered, then opens the viewer. It refuses to
pull over local changes or another branch. If Git Bash is not in
its standard location, set `OSTROG_GIT_BASH` to the full path of `bash.exe`.

Then run the client from the repository root:

    tools/godot --path godot                    # windowed, hands-on
    tools/godot --path godot -- --seed=7 --fixed-seed   # skip the autosaves; the wizard on seed 7

After regenerating art or sound, run one editor import pass so Godot does not draw stale
textures: `tools/godot --path godot --headless --editor --quit` (`tools/shots/shot.sh` does this
by itself when any asset is newer than its stamp).

Regenerating assets:

| Command | Writes |
|---|---|
| `tools/art/build_all.sh` | palette, terrain, overlays, structures, plants, items, light; then palette check, provenance table and contact sheets in `shots/out/` |
| `python3 tools/art/people.py` | people layer sheets and `swaps.json` (not run by build_all.sh) |
| `python3 tools/art/animals.py` | animal sheets (not run by build_all.sh) |
| `python3 tools/audio/sfx.py` | every OGG in `godot/assets/audio/` and `sounds.json` |
| `python3 tools/art/palette_check.py` | fails if any opaque pixel is off the 92-colour palette |
| `python3 tools/art/contact_sheet.py`, `people_contact.py` | review sheets in `shots/out/` |
| `python3 tools/art/value_report.py` | luminance per terrain family and which families read alike |
| `python3 tools/art/contrast_report.py` | face feature contrast per skin tone (exit 1 under the minimum) |
| `python3 tools/audio/audio_report.py [OUT.png]` | levels and loop seams per OGG, spectrogram sheet |
| `python3 tools/art/provenance_report.py` | refreshes the counts table in ASSETS.md |

### Tests and CI

    tools/ci.sh

CI runs on the build box, not on GitHub: GitHub Actions is manual-only, and the result of a
commit is its `box-ci` status. A runner in a Docker container (`hf-boxci`, tools/boxci/README.md)
tests the master head and the head of every open pull request with `tools/ci.sh`, one at a
time (master first, then pull requests oldest first, about 10 minutes each), and posts
`pending`, then `success` or `failure`. Without access to the box,
push a branch and open a pull request, then read `gh pr checks <number>` or
`gh api repos/danhicks96/ostrog/commits/<sha>/statuses`. On the box, run `tools/ci.sh`, or
`docker exec hf-boxci runner.sh run <sha>` for a pushed commit. Nothing merges to master without
`box-ci` success on a pull request head that contains origin/master; `tools/merge/gated_merge.sh`
checks both (tools/boxci/README.md, "Merging").

`tools/ci.sh` runs, in order: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D
warnings`, the tests (`cargo nextest run --workspace` and `cargo test --workspace --doc` when
cargo-nextest is installed, otherwise `cargo test --workspace`; tools/boxci/README.md, "Parallel
tests"), `python3 -I tools/art/provenance_check.py` (every file
under `godot/assets` needs a provenance row), `python3 tools/audio/audio_report.py` (fails on
clipping or a click at a loop seam). It prints `CI OK` at the end. A run of only one gate file:

    cargo test --release -p hf-sim --test techniques
    cargo test --release -p hf-sim --test foundations digest_deterministic

Integration tests live in `crates/hf-sim/tests/`, one file per area:

| File | Gates |
|---|---|
| autonomy.rs | need_satisfaction_seeded, knowledge_only_targets |
| aversion.rs | bad places avoided locally, trauma fades, someone who hurt you is avoided (8 tests) |
| beliefs.rs | belief_has_sources, weight_stays_within_the_cap, beliefs_spread_by_talk, outcomes_confirm_and_shake, children_start_with_their_parents_lightly, ideology_is_beliefs_not_rules |
| craft.rs | recipe_chain_ore_to_tool, quality_affects_use, worn_out_replaced |
| culture.rs | custom_origin_traceable, custom_changes_behavior |
| depth.rs | dig_reveals_cave_event, underground_cellar_in_use |
| engine.rs | the Engine is deterministic, differs by mode, fires no events, travels with the save (8 tests) |
| expedition.rs | caravan_returns_with_outcome, caravan_lost_leaves_history, expedition_memories_consolidate |
| foundations.rs | digest_deterministic, save_roundtrip_identical, save_manifest_mismatch_rejected, save_newer_format_refused, worldgen_has_the_expected_features, interrupted_write_recovers, migration_chain_loads_fixture |
| generations.rs | genome_vs_learned_separation, three_generations_lineage |
| hazards.rs | flood_fills_basin, fire_spreads_and_is_put_out, cold_without_clothes_hurts, fire_spreads_unchecked |
| health.rs | wound_heals_with_rest, infection_can_kill, illness_spreads_then_fades |
| history.rs | chronicle_only_real_events, history_view_reads_the_record |

| memory.rs | memory_changes_choice, memory_cap_bounded, relationship_from_memories |
| missing.rs | the seven have seeded fates, word sends someone looking, searching resolves it (9 tests) |
| movement.rs | walks_a_straight_path_at_walk_speed, blocked_tile_stops_the_path_and_flags_it |
| navigation.rs | vertical_path_via_stairs, unreachable_detected_o1, region_rebuild_after_wall, path_throughput_on_generated_world, stairs_link_both_ways |
| opening.rs | ten generated and three begin, rerolling one slot changes only that slot, the card is the survivor you get (8 tests) |
| past.rs | past_is_recorded_history, ages_match_the_lives_they_lived, siblings_diverge, past_shapes_but_does_not_decide, rerolling_keeps_the_family, epoch_zero_unchanged, the_calendar_counts_from_the_arrival |
| search_loop.rs | someone_goes_and_finds_out, no_word_means_nobody_goes |

| weather_events.rs | wet_weather_turning_is_an_event |
| wildlife.rs | wildlife_persists_and_moves, hunting_feeds_the_community |
| work.rs | prereq_chain_cook_from_harvest, contention_no_deadlock, lease_expiry_sweep, interrupted_plan_releases_and_keeps_items, target_vanishes_recovers, ledger_zero_drift_default_world |

### The ostrog CLI

`cargo run --release -p hf-cli -- <command>` or `target/release/ostrog <command>`.
Every command takes `--help`. Without `--opening`, `run` and `soak` build the old test valley
with `World::generate` (and, with `--agents N`, a founding party of N): no portal, no past and
no neighbours. With `--opening` they run the game as it is played, from the arrival. `legends`,
`realm` and `past` make the wider world.

| Command | Options (defaults) | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| `run` | `--seed 1`, `--ticks 28800` (one game-day) or `--days N`, `--opening`, `--small`, `--save PATH`, `--agents 0`, `--engine steady\|relentless\|whimsical`, `--debug-log PATH` (or env `OSTROG_DEBUG_LOG`: JSON Lines, a header then every decision with its scored options, failed and abandoned plans, and a counter line per game day; 32 MiB cap, then `PATH.1`) | generate a world, run it, print plan failures, open designations, structures, ledger drift, every agent's needs and activity (`STUCK` lines for anyone in one activity over 6,000 ticks), event tallies, digest and ms per tick. With `--agents 0` the world is empty. |
| `soak` | `--seed 1`, `--days 600`, `--agents 12`, `--csv PATH`, `--ring DIR`, `--save-days 5`, `--opening`, `--peoples N` (with `--opening`), `--provision` (without `--opening`: a day's meals each midnight, as `profile --provision`; #369), `--engine steady\|relentless\|whimsical` (which deity paces the soaked world; default steady; #1505) | long run with per-day metrics (tick p50/p99, stalled people, plan failures, leases expired, ledger drift, memories, chronicle rows, RSS) and a final threshold report; `--peoples` also prints a tally of the peoples' events |
| `weather` | `--seed 1`, `--days 30` | sky and season changes per day, to find a rainy or snowy hour for a shot |
| `profile` | `--seed 1`, `--days 60`, `--agents 12`, `--spike-ms 5`, `--provision`, `--mixed` | per-system tick timings and which systems own the spikes; `--provision` stocks meals for the whole party so the load stays at its size, `--mixed` makes the party of every kind at once |
| `director` | `--seed 7`, `--start 288000`, `--every 1200`, `--frames 30`, `--agents 12` | the camera director's suggestion at each frame, with who is in frame |
| `digest` | `--seed 1`, `--small` | digest of a freshly generated world at tick 0 |
| `resume` | `PATH`, `--ticks 0` | load a save, run more ticks, print the digest |
| `verify` | `PATH` | check a save's manifest and hashes; prints `ok format N game V seed S tick T` |
| `stage` | `--seed 1` | digest of the staged demo scene (art review only) |
| `bench-save` | `--seed 1`, `--ticks 160000` | time the parts of a save |
| `fixture` | `PATH` | write a small save fixture for the migration test |
| `debug-plan` | `--seed 1`, `--ticks 57600` | try to plan every open designation for every person |
| `tile` | `X Y`, `--seed 1` | dump one column of tiles |
| `describe` | `--seed 1`, `--small`, `--wetness W`, `--warmth W`, `--stoniness S` (each -1 to 1) | summarise a generated world: character, biome mix, ground and plant families with why each is there or not, terrain, water, founding site |
| `legends` | `--seed 1`, `--page world:0` | print a page of the Legends browser for a new opening world: `world:0`, `people:N`, `site:N`, `figure:N` or `polity:N` |
| `realm` | `--seed 1`, `--size 192`, `--ppm PATH`, `--years 0` | the world's region map: biome mix, rivers, lakes; `--years` runs the peoples and their settlements that long; `--ppm` writes the map as a picture |
| `past` | `--seed 1`, `--engine steady\|relentless\|whimsical`, `--years 150` | make an opening world from new-game choices and print its past: the old hamlets, each time the deity leaned on one, the patterns the valley's people noticed |

Determinism check: `ostrog run --seed 7 --ticks 200000 --agents 12` twice; the last line,
`digest <hex>`, must match. Without `--agents` the world has no people, so the digest only
covers terrain, plants and animals.

### Client command-line flags

Parsed by `_parse_args()` in `godot/scripts/main.gd`. They go after `--` on the Godot command
line (`tools/godot --path godot -- --seed=7`). Anything not in this table prints
`UNKNOWN_FLAG <flag> (ignored)`.

| Flag | Effect |
|---|---|
| `--seed=N` | world seed (default 1). Any `--seed` skips the autosaves and starts a new valley: on a hands-on launch the arrival screen, on a fresh random valley unless `--fixed-seed` is given too (#559); in a shot, footage, `--headless`, `--save-at-end` or `--wizard-shot` run, a valley for that seed. |
| `--fixed-seed` | the arrival screen opens on `--seed` (default 1) instead of a fresh random valley |
| `--fresh` | ignore the autosaves and start the arrival without the wizard, paced steadily (seed 1 unless `--seed`) |
| `--sim-inline` | step the sim on the main thread as before #603, not on its own thread (for debugging; `HF_SIM_THREAD=0` does the same) |
| `--opening=DEITY` | start from the arrival without the wizard: the first three of the ten cards, paced by `steady`, `relentless` or `whimsical` (Aun, Hask, Mirre). Starts fresh; combine with `--seed=N`. |
| `--load=PATH` | load this `.hfsave` directory |
| `--ticks=N` or `--steps=N` | run N ticks before the first frame (the same flag; `--ticks` is the CLI's word) |
| `--ignite=X,Y` | after the world exists and the `--ticks` have run, set the surface at tile X,Y alight (prints `IGNITE (X, Y) -> true/false`; false means nothing there burns) |
| `--after-ignite=N` | ticks to run after `--ignite` (default 160) |
| `--speed=N` | starting speed index: 0 paused, 1 = 1x (default), 2 = 3x, 3 = 10x |
| `--z=N` | view level (default: the settlement's level) |
| `--zoom=F` | camera zoom (default 1.0; the wheel steps through 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4) |
| `--at=X,Y` | centre the camera on tile X,Y |
| `--at=band` | centre the camera on the first band of strangers' camp, on its level (prints `BANDS [...]`) |
| `--hungry-neighbour` | before the run, empty the stores of one place beyond the valley (prints `HUNGRY ...`); whether and when its people come is still the simulation's |
| `--legends=PAGE` | open the Legends browser at PAGE (`world:0`, `people:N`, `site:N`, `figure:N`, `polity:N`) |
| `--music-fast` | music director: change pieces quickly (for checking music transitions) |
| `--verbose-audio` | print the music director's lines (cues, phrases) to the console and godot.log; they are off by default because they were most of godot.log (#565) |
| `--debug-log[=PATH]` | write the debug log, JSON Lines, to PATH (default `ostrog-debug.log` in the user data folder). Line 1 is the header: seed, git commit, game version, world, region, history years, settings and command line. Then one line per decision (`decide`: agent, chosen goal, every option with its score and whether it was available), per failed plan (`plan_failed`, with the reason), per abandoned plan (`abandoned`), and one `day` line per game day with counters (plans made / failed / abandoned, needs crossing into starving, parched, exhausted). Capped at 32 MiB, then moved to `PATH.1`. Env `OSTROG_DEBUG_LOG=PATH` and the "Debug decision log" checkbox in settings (O) do the same. Prints `DEBUG_LOG on: PATH`. Changes nothing in the simulation. For a watch session: `OSTROG_DEBUG_LOG=1 tools/watch-copy.sh`, or `tools/watch-copy.sh -- --debug-log` (#565) |
| `--debug-hud` | show the developer readings on the HUD line beside "<deity> watches": the engine's strain (`strain N%`) and the simulation step time (`step X ms`). Off by default, so the HUD names only the watching deity (#1740). Changes nothing in the simulation |
| `--inspect=ID` | open the inspect panel on agent ID and centre on them |
| `--why` | with `--inspect`, open the inspect panel's Why? lens (#567) |
| `--inspect-block=X,Y[,Z]` | open block inspection at the given tile; omit Z to inspect the surface |
| `--favourite=ID[,ID...]` | mark the listed people as favourites before the run |
| `--favourites` | open the favourites panel at start |
| `--people` | open the People panel at start (everyone of the valley, here or away) |
| `--waiting` | open the Waiting work panel at start (work nobody has taken, and why) |
| `--hide-bar` | start with the mouse command bar hidden |
| `--feed-rate=MODE` | start with the feed rate set to `calm` (`0`), `normal` (`1`), or `full` (`2`) (#413) |
| `--first-run-hints` | show the step-by-step first-run hints on the HUD even in a shot or when previously seen (#413) |
| `--history` | open the History panel at start |
| `--known` | start in the "what the community has seen" view (V) |
| `--rel-lines` | start with the relationship-lines overlay on (R; #192, WP-08 8.4) |
| `--no-director` | camera director off |
| `--shot=PATH` | after the ticks, save one PNG of the window to PATH and quit (`SHOT_SAVED PATH`). Use an absolute path: a relative one resolves against `godot/`. |
| `--footage=DIR` | save `DIR/f00.png`, `f01.png`, ... with the director driving the camera, then quit (`FOOTAGE_FRAME n reason`, `FOOTAGE_DONE`). DIR must exist. |
| `--every=N` | ticks between footage frames |
| `--frames=N` | number of footage frames |
| `--save-at-end=PATH` | write a save at PATH once the run is set up and quit (`SAVE PATH -> true (ok, capture N ms)`); with `--shot` it saves before the picture |
| `--automation-json=PATH` | write a versioned JSON result for a deterministic inspect/shot smoke run, including world seed, tick, selected inspect data and output paths; quit after writing when no shot or footage is requested |
| `--audio-log=PATH` | let shot and footage runs play sound (they are silent otherwise) and write each sound played, as `seconds name x y`, to PATH |
| `--wizard-shot=PATH` | step through the wizard, saving each page as `PATH_1.png` to `PATH_6.png`, press Begin, save `PATH_started.png`, quit |
| `--wizard-choices=K=V,...` | with `--wizard-shot`, choose first: `deity=Hask`, `years=300`, `held=strangers:-1`, `exclude=Tharn`, `rarity=Skarri:rare` |
| `--stage=demo`, `peoples`, `walls`, `homes` | a staged scene for art review (decision D13): the representative scene, every kind of people, the wall materials, the homes |
| `--ring` | in a shot or footage run, load the newest autosave instead of starting fresh |

`--shot`, `--footage` and `--wizard-shot` need a renderer. Under Godot's `--headless` nothing
is ever drawn, so `--shot` prints `SHOT_FAILED ... --headless draws nothing` and exits with
code 2. Run them windowed (on Windows any window size works, e.g. `--resolution 640x360`; on
Linux use `tools/shots/shot.sh`, which draws on Xvfb). Runs that only save are fine headless:

    tools/godot --path godot --headless -- --opening=steady --ticks=28800 --save-at-end=/tmp/day1.hfsave
    target/release/ostrog verify /tmp/day1.hfsave

### How the client decides what world to start

`_ready()` in main.gd, in this order:

1. `--stage=...`: the staged scene.
2. `--opening=DEITY`: the arrival with the first three cards, no wizard.
3. `--load=PATH`: that save (falls back to the arrival if it fails).
4. The newest valid autosave, unless any of `--fresh`, `--seed`, `--wizard-shot`, `--opening`
   is given or this is a shot or footage run without `--ring`.
5. Otherwise, on a hands-on launch (a window, and no shot, footage, `--save-at-end`, fresh,
   stage or load): the wizard, on a fresh random valley each New game, or on `--seed=N`
   (default 1) with `--fixed-seed` or `--wizard-shot` (#559). Going back through its pages
   keeps the valley and the place chosen on the world map. Otherwise the arrival, paced
   steadily, with no wizard (#104).

So `tools/godot --path godot -- --seed=N --fixed-seed` opens the wizard for seed N, and `--fresh` or
`--opening=steady --seed=N` starts the arrival on any seed without it.

### Screenshots, footage and saves without a window

    tools/shots/shot.sh OUT.png [client flags...]
    tools/shots/shot.sh /tmp/fire.png --seed=1 --ticks=28800 --ignite=48,26 --after-ignite=600

`shot.sh` re-imports assets when any PNG or OGG is newer than its stamp or the import cache is
incomplete, runs the client on display `HF_DISPLAY` (default `:96`, an Xvfb server you start
yourself, e.g. `Xvfb :96 -screen 0 1600x900x24 &`) with `--rendering-driver opengl3`, waits up
to `HF_SHOT_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 120), and writes the client's log next to the PNG
(`OUT.log`). It exits 1 with the first errors if the log has no `SHOT_SAVED`.

The import pass (`tools/godot --path godot --headless --editor --quit`) crashes now and then
inside Godot 4.6.3 itself (issue 42): the threaded reimport logs "The caller thread can't call
the function `propagate_notification()`" for `/root` and dies with SIGSEGV, about once in 20
fresh imports, leaving the cache half-written. So `shot.sh` checks every asset against Godot's
own import records (`.godot/imported/*.md5`) rather than trusting the exit code, runs the pass
a second time if anything is missing, and stops with `shot FAILED: the asset import is still
incomplete after a retry` rather than shoot from a broken cache. The pass's output is kept in
`godot/.godot/hf_import.log` (`hf_import.retry.log` for the second pass). The client also no
longer draws flat fallback colours over a half-written cache: when an imported texture cannot
be loaded it reads the PNG directly (`assets.gd`).

Footage has no wrapper script; call the client directly with `--footage=DIR --every=N
--frames=N`.

### Packaging, checkpoints, restore

| Script | What it does |
|---|---|
| `watch-copy.cmd` | Windows shortcut target: on a clean `master`, fast-forward pulls, installs the exact commit's checksum-verified prebuilt DLL or builds it locally, then opens the viewer; refuses dirty and non-master checkouts |
| `tools/package.sh [VERSION]` | needs the Linux/Windows Godot bridge and `hf-launcher` binaries, the 4.6.3 export templates, `OSTROG_MANIFEST_URL` and `OSTROG_PUBLIC_KEY`; writes app archives plus portable launcher packages with `app/`, sibling `data/`, `launcher.toml` and checksums under `releases/`, prints `PACKAGE OK <version>` |
| `tools/release/sign_manifest.sh VERSION URL ARCHIVE KEY OUTPUT` | signs the exact Windows x64 app archive with an external raw/hex Ed25519 key and writes the public JSON manifest; the key is never shipped; see `docs/release-updater.md` |
| `tools/release/publish_windows.sh TAG MANIFEST ARCHIVE PORTABLE CHECKSUMS NOTES` | validates the signed manifest, trusted Ed25519 signature and exact Windows archive, then publishes the four named assets through `gh release create --verify-tag` using CI-only `GH_TOKEN` and an external public-key file |
| `tools/server/pull_sign_publish.sh TAG VERSION` | server-side release step: checks the tag is on master, pulls the app archive from the private release, signs and verifies it with the server-held key through a prebuilt `hf-release`, then publishes archive and manifest to the served directory; config in `tools/server/README.md` |
| `tools/checkpoint.sh "message"` | the box's verified checkpoint: `tools/ci.sh`, `git add -A` and commit, push with retries, bundle, rsync the source and releases to the backup mirror, verify the mirror, write `CHECKPOINT.txt`; prints `CHECKPOINT OK <commit>` or leaves `MIRROR_PENDING.md` |
| `tools/prebuilt/windows_dll.sh [COMMIT]` | cross-builds the release `hf_godot.dll` for a commit (default `origin/master`) in a worktree on the NVMe, checks it is an x86-64 PE exporting the gdextension entry symbol, publishes it to `prebuilt/windows/<commit>/` on the mirror with a sha256, moves `latest.txt` forward, keeps the newest 10 and moves older ones to `to_delete/`; prints `PREBUILT OK <commit>` or leaves `PREBUILT_PENDING.md`. `checkpoint.sh` runs it on master after its verify, and its failure does not fail the checkpoint |
| `tools/checkpoint-windows.sh "message"` | the same contract on the Windows machine, mirroring with robocopy |
| `tools/restore_test.sh [DIR]` | clones the mirror's bundle into a scratch directory and runs `tools/ci.sh` there; prints `RESTORE OK` |
| `tools/boxci/` | the box CI runner: Dockerfile, `runner.sh` (fetch every 3 minutes, run `tools/ci.sh` on the master head and open pull request heads, post the `box-ci` status), `compose.yaml`; how to start and stop it in its README |

`checkpoint.sh` commits everything in the working tree and pushes the current branch; it is
for the project's own sessions, not for testers.

### Keyboard and mouse

From `_unhandled_input()` and `_camera()` in main.gd. `?` shows the key list (it opens by
itself once, on the first hands-on launch).

| Input | Does |
|---|---|
| Space | pause; pressed again it resumes at 1x (not at the previous speed) |
| 1, 2, 3 | 1x, 3x, 10x |
| `,` or PageDown / `.` or PageUp | view one level down / up |
| W A S D or arrow keys (held) | pan |
| right or middle mouse drag | pan |
| mouse wheel | zoom in / out through 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, toward the pointer |
| left click on a person | inspect them (click empty ground to clear) |
| left click on a feed line | look where it happened, on its level |
| ? | the key list; ? or Esc closes it |
| F | follow the inspected person; panning (keys or a drag), a bookmark jump, Esc or clicking empty ground stops it, a plain mouse move does not |
| C | camera director on / off |
| H | History panel; H again closes it (also closes the away summary) |
| L | Legends browser (the wider world); Backspace goes back a page, L or Esc closes it |
| V | toggle "what the community has seen" / "everything" |
| P | People panel: everyone of the valley, here or away, what they are doing and why; Inspect or Follow from the list; P or Esc closes it |
| K | Waiting work: every order nobody has taken for an hour or more, and why; Go to looks there; K or Esc closes it |
| F5 | manual save to `saves/manual.hfsave` |
| F9 | load the manual save |
| Ctrl+F1..F4, Ctrl+F6..F8 | set a bookmark (camera position, level, zoom) |
| F1..F4, F6..F8 | go to that bookmark (F5 is save, so there is no bookmark 5) |
| O | settings |
| Esc | close the History or away summary, else settings, else clear the selection and follow |

Known control quirks on master, worth knowing before filing:

- The director takes the camera back 60 real seconds after the last key, click or mouse move.
- The away summary opens when the window regains focus after more than 1,200 ticks (one
  game-hour) away.

### The screen

HUD (`_update_hud`): `{valley}   Year {Y} {Season} day {D}  {HH:MM}   z {level}   pop
{humans}   {Community} {N} ({n} on a journey)   {speed}   director: on, watching {what} | off
{deity} watches  strain {S}%   step {ms} ms   [?] keys   {note}`, where `{what}` is what the
camera went to look at in plain words ("the community", "a fire", "people at work"). Lines
starting `!` are a band of strangers or troubles piling up. A last line names whoever the
pointer is over: `> {name}  {activity}   (click to inspect)`, or the house under it. The HUD
wraps before it reaches the inspect panel.

Feed (bottom left): the last nine lines of the past 45 seconds, in a box as tall as its lines.
Grey lines are minor and throttled to one per 0.7 real seconds, and "+N quieter lines" counts
those let pass; yellow are notable; red are major. Click a line to look where it happened.

Inspect panel (right): `{name}  {Species}, {age} years`, activity, community and role, bars
for hunger, rest, social, comfort, warmth, traits, skills above 0.5, partner, parents,
children, customs, mood (cheerful above 0.3, content above 0, low above -0.3, else
miserable), people (`friend`, `likes`, `knows`, `dislikes` with opinion), believes, "before the
portal, in {home}:", remembers, and "knows N things" with up to twelve ("oak logs", "a hearth",
a name). It scrolls when it is longer than the screen. The bridge also sends
body, thoughts, stress, tool and clothes wear; the panel does not draw those yet.

History (H): eras by year (counts of born, died, arrived, left, joined, turned away, built,
split, trade, custom formed, caravan returned, caravan lost, hunted, cave in, paired), "The
record" (the latest 14 major lines, `Y{year} day {day} {HH:MM}`, with detail lines indented),
and "Why they do what they do" (each custom with its holders, strength and the recorded line
it grew from).

While you were away: counts by chronicle kind since focus was lost and up to twelve major
lines, `Day {day} {HH:MM}`.

Settings (O): Master, Ambient, Effects and Music volume, autosave interval in game-days (0 to
5, 0 is off, default 1), camera director, and the save folder path. Stored in
`user://settings.cfg` with the bookmarks.

The wizard (`setup.gd`): "Ostrog: {page}" over the six page names, Back and Next (Begin on
the last page). Next on "Who came through" needs exactly three chosen; Begin is disabled, with
the reason, when no kind left in could live above ground. A world that cannot be made as
chosen stays on the wizard at the page concerned, with the reason in red (#147).

The dead lie where they fell: falling, then lying, for three game-hours when their people lay
them to rest and a day when nobody does.

## 3. Glossary and every string the game shows

### Terms

The owner chose the player-facing words (docs/design-glossary.md); internal type names stay
as they are.

| Term | Meaning | In code |
|---|---|---|
| survivor | one of the ten who came through the portal. The word is used in docs and code, not on screen: the screen shows names. | `Agent` of `Species::Human`; `opening::SurvivorSlot` |
| the ten, the party | all who came through (`PARTY = 10`) | `opening::PARTY` |
| starting survivors, the three | the three who begin together at the landing site (`STARTING = 3`) | `SurvivorState::Starting` |
| the missing | the other seven, scattered on the map, alive or dead, unknown until someone goes and looks. About 55% are alive, decided by seed and hidden. "The lost" is not used for them; "lost" in the game means a caravan lost on the road. | `SurvivorState::Missing { truth }` |
| rumour, word | a stranger's word that someone matching one of the ten was seen somewhere; a reason to go and look, not knowledge. Goes cold after 20 days. | `opening::Rumour`, `hear_of` |
| search | someone setting out to look ("set out to look for {name}, word came they were seen that way"), settled when they reach the place: found alive and coming back, found dead, or no sign | `opening::Search`, `resolve_search` |
| the portal, the arrival | the event that starts the game; also the setup screen ("Ostrog — the arrival") | `scenario::opening_world_with`, `godot/scripts/setup.gd` |
| epoch | the tick the portal opens: a whole number of years after the world's tick 0, late enough for the oldest survivor's life to fit before it (decision D18) | `WorldMeta.epoch`, `past::epoch` |
| before the portal, past, lived life | each survivor's life year by year in an old-world village, recorded as chronicle rows dated before the epoch | `past.rs`, `Formative`, `EventKind::Lived` |
| home, the old village | the village a survivor came from (inspect: "before the portal, in {home}") | `Agent.home` |
| the Engine | the pacing model chosen at the arrival: Steady, Relentless or Whimsical. It changes how often strangers come and how choosy the welcome is; it fires no events. Never named in the world's voice. | `engine.rs` |
| strain | the Engine's reading of how hard things are, 0 to 100% (needs unmet, work backlog) | `engine::tension` |
| community | a named group with a home, members, roles and a welcome norm. Names are generated per seed from two syllable lists (seed 1's first community is Oakfolk), not fixed. | `society.rs`, `names::community_name` |
| valley, world name | the map's generated name (seed 1: Ashcross) | `names::world_name` |
| newcomer, stranger | someone who turns up at the valley edge and is taken in or turned away by the welcome norm | `systems/society.rs` |
| welcome norm | WhenFed (default: while there is a day of food per head), Open, Closed | `WelcomeNorm` |
| role | daily work by skill: forager, builder, farmer, cook, miner, hauler, healer | `Role` |
| traits | ten temperaments: Industrious, Easygoing, Sociable, Solitary, Curious, Cautious, Hearty, Gourmet, LightSleeper, Tidy | `mind/traits.rs` |
| skills | nine, 0 to 20 (work speed x0.6 at 0 to x1.8 at 20): Foraging, Farming, Cooking, Construction, Mining, Crafting, Social, Hauling, Animals | `mind/skills.rs` |
| needs | hunger, rest, warmth, social, comfort (inspect bars) | `mind/needs.rs` |
| memory | consolidated in sleep; biases food, place and partner choices; fond, sour or plain | `mind/memory.rs` |
| belief | a stance on a topic (drink, violence, fire, hunger, strangers, death, trust) with a confidence and sources: what happened, who told them, what their people held. Weights a choice between x0.6 and x1.4, never decides it. | `mind/beliefs.rs` |
| technique | a way of doing something a person knows or does not: beating out flames, carrying water to a fire, a poultice, foraging far afield. Learned from the past, by watching it work, or by trying it. Replaces crisis orders: nobody is sent to a fire. | `mind/techniques.rs`, `systems/learn.rs` |
| thought | a weighted, dated feeling (ate well, grieving, saw fire) feeding mood and stress | `mind/thoughts.rs` |
| break | what too much stress does: a tantrum, withdrawal, wandering off, a fight; witnesses remember it | `systems/strain.rs` |
| custom | something a community holds and acts on, grown from a recorded event, with a narrative: rest days, food taboos, storing food, a changed welcome | `culture.rs` |
| real history, taught history | the chronicle of what happened, and the versions of it people pass on; customs join the two | `chronicle/`, D9 |
| chronicle | the SQLite record of every event; history and the away summary read only from it | `chronicle/mod.rs` |
| era | a year's counts in the History panel | `view/history.rs` |
| caravan, party, journey | up to three who leave the map to trade or scout and come back, or are lost | `expedition.rs`, `overworld.away` |
| site | a place beyond the map edge (Greywater, Tollmoor, Hollin's Rest, Brackenford, Saltmere, Oldstone, Fennwick, Thornhallow, Redcroft, Mirewell) | `expedition.rs` |
| designation, work order | a job posted by the community planner (wall, field, shaft, craft...) and taken up as a plan | `work/designation.rs`, `work/planner.rs` |
| lease | a person's claim on items or a site while they work, all or nothing, expiring | `work/reservation.rs` |
| ledger | the conservation audit of every item; drift is a bug | `economy.rs` |
| quality tier | 0 crude, 1 poor, 2 plain, 3 good, 4 fine, 5 masterly | `ItemKind::tier_name` |
| the director | the camera director (not the Engine): it moves the camera to where things are happening | `view/director.rs` |
| the feed | the scrolling event lines bottom left | `view/narrate.rs` |
| known view | V: draws only cells the first community has stood in | `SimHost.explored` |

Words the game does not use on screen: pawn, colonist, settler, dwarf, duplicant, storyteller
(for the Engine), "the lost ones" (for the missing). This must come back empty:
`grep -rniE 'pawn|colonist|settler|\bdwarf' godot/scripts/ godot/scenes/`.

### Every string the simulation can show

Generated from `crates/hf-sim/src` by `tools/docs/readme_tables.py`. `{field}` placeholders
name the event field or argument that fills them.

#### Feed, history and away-summary lines (`view/narrate.rs` `line`)

One row per `EventKind`. `{name}` is filled from the event: a person's or community's name, or the recorded field of that name. The chronicle kind is what the away summary counts and what `ostrog run` tallies. Severity is the level written at its push sites (a level chosen at run time shows as computed): Trace never reaches the feed, Minor is grey and throttled, Notable is yellow, Major is red and also goes to History and the director. Lines dated before the arrival (`Lived`) appear only in the inspect panel and History, never in the feed.

#### Belief phrases (`mind/beliefs.rs` `phrase`)

Inspect shows each belief as `{phrase} ({certainty}; from {sources})`. Certainty: "surely" (confidence > 0.7), "fairly sure" (> 0.4), otherwise "half-believes". Sources: "what happened" / "N things that happened", "told by {name}", "what their people held". Beliefs with |stance| x confidence under 0.08 are not shown.

| topic | stance | phrase |
|---|---|---|
| Drink | below 0 | drink is ruin |
| Drink | 0 or above | drink eases things |
| Violence | below 0 | you never raise a hand |
| Violence | 0 or above | a fist settles things |
| Fire | below 0 | a fire is something you fight |
| Fire | 0 or above | a fire is something you run from |
| Hunger | below 0 | in hunger you share |
| Hunger | 0 or above | in hunger you keep what you have |
| Strangers | below 0 | strangers are welcome |
| Strangers | 0 or above | strangers bring harm |
| Death | below 0 | grief is borne alone |
| Death | 0 or above | the living must be held close |
| Trust | below 0 | people deserve forgiving |
| Trust | 0 or above | trust is earned slowly |
| Kill | below 0 | killing outside a fight is abhorrent |
| Kill | 0 or above | some deserve killing |
| EatDead | below 0 | eating the dead is abhorrent |
| EatDead | 0 or above | the dead may feed the living |
| Theft | below 0 | taking what is another's is wrong |
| Theft | 0 or above | what lies about is anyone's |
| CutLivingTree | below 0 | a living tree is not to be cut |
| CutLivingTree | 0 or above | a tree is timber |
| Burial | below 0 | how the dead are kept matters little |
| Burial | 0 or above | the dead must be given their rite |
| OtherRaces | below 0 | other kinds are best kept apart |
| OtherRaces | 0 or above | any kind may live among us |
| DeitySigns | below 0 | signs are only weather |
| DeitySigns | 0 or above | the signs must be heeded |
| DeepDigging | below 0 | the deep is not to be dug |
| DeepDigging | 0 or above | the deep is there to be dug |
| KeepingBeasts | below 0 | beasts should run free |
| KeepingBeasts | 0 or above | beasts are for keeping |
| Tradition | below 0 | the old ways bind no one |
| Tradition | 0 or above | the old ways are to be kept |
| Family | below 0 | kin are no closer than anyone |
| Family | 0 or above | kin come first |
| Craftsmanship | below 0 | a thing need only work |
| Craftsmanship | 0 or above | a thing well made is worth the making |
| Knowledge | below 0 | some things are better not known |
| Knowledge | 0 or above | what can be learned should be |
| Commerce | below 0 | trading is for the greedy |
| Commerce | 0 or above | a fair trade helps both |
| Nature | below 0 | the wild is to be tamed |
| Nature | 0 or above | the wild is to be left be |
| Peace | below 0 | peace is for the weak |
| Peace | 0 or above | peace is worth keeping |
| MartialProwess | below 0 | fighting is no one's pride |
| MartialProwess | 0 or above | skill at arms is to be honoured |
| Merriment | below 0 | merriment is idleness |
| Merriment | 0 or above | a merry night is good for everyone |
| Tranquility | below 0 | noise is the sound of living |

| Hospitality | below 0 | a guest is a burden |
| Hospitality | 0 or above | a guest is to be fed |

#### Activities (`mind/activity.rs` `describe`)

The second line of the inspect panel and the hover line in the HUD. `{name}` in "heading off to" is the next activity's short name: idle, going, eating, foraging, sleeping, talking, loitering, wandering, working, drinking.

| Activity | text |
|---|---|
| Idle | standing about |
| Going | heading off to {then} |
| Eating | eating |
| Foraging | gathering food |
| Sleeping | asleep in bed |
| Sleeping | asleep on the ground |
| Talking | talking |
| Loitering | resting a moment |
| Wandering | wandering |
| Working | (the work plan's label, below) |
| Drinking | drinking |

#### Customs (`culture.rs` `label`, `systems/culture.rs` narratives)

Feed: "{community} now hold that we {label}: \"{narrative}\"". Inspect lists "{label} — {narrative}", with " (not yet taken up)" for members who have not adopted it. History: "Why they do what they do".

| CustomKind | label |
|---|---|
| RestDay | rest on day {day_of_season} of each season |
| FoodTaboo | eat no {label} |
| StoreFood | store food against hunger |
| HearthNight | gather at the hearth at dusk |
| Building | build of {m} and roof with {roof} / roof with {roof} / thatch / shingles |
| UpperStorey | build upward when another room is needed |
| Welcome Open | take in every stranger |
| Welcome Closed | take in no strangers |
| Welcome WhenFed | take in strangers when fed |

Narratives:

| where | narrative |
|---|---|
| systems/culture.rs:498 | On the {ordinal} day of each season we rest, because {who} died that day, {cause}. |
| systems/culture.rs:518 | We sow and cook more than we need, because {who} starved. |
| systems/culture.rs:534 | We take in no strangers, since {who} led people away: {cause}. |
| systems/culture.rs:570 | We do not eat {k}, because {who} fell ill of it and went hungry. |

#### Enum words shown as they are

The inspect panel and arrival cards print traits and skills by their Rust names (e.g. "LightSleeper", "Construction 7"); roles in lower case; memories as "{MemKind} {subject} ({fond|sour|plain}, {strength}%)".

| Role (society.rs) | Forager, Builder, Farmer, Cook, Miner, Hauler, Healer |

| Skill (mind/skills.rs) | Foraging, Farming, Cooking, Construction, Mining, Crafting, Social, Hauling, Animals |
| Topic (past.rs) | Drink, Violence, Fire, Hunger, Strangers, Death, Trust, Kill, EatDead, Theft, CutLivingTree, Burial, OtherRaces, DeitySigns, DeepDigging, KeepingBeasts, Tradition, Family, Craftsmanship, Knowledge, Commerce, Nature, Peace, MartialProwess, Merriment, Tranquility, Hospitality |
| Formative (past.rs) | ParentDrank, ParentViolent, ParentDied, SiblingDied, KindElder, Famine, HouseFire, VillageFire, Raid, Fever, Flood, Apprenticed, Married, ChildBorn, ChildDied, Betrayed, SavedFromWater, LostInWoods, FoughtFire, Answered, LeftBehind |

#### Name parts (`names.rs`)

People, valleys and communities are named from these syllables by seed, so any name on screen is made of them.

| array | parts |
|---|---|
| ONSETS | Ael Bran Cor Dael Eir Fen Gal Hal Ir Kel Lir Mor Nal Or Per Ros Sae Tor Ul Vel Wyn Yl |
| MIDS | a e i o u ae ei ou |
| CODAS | dric wen lin mar ric sa thi vane wick ya nor del sin gar la n |
| WORLD_A | Hearth Ash Fern Stone Mere Holt Wyn Thorn Elder Reed |
| WORLD_B | fall dale reach mere hollow moor bank fold wold cross |
| COMM_A | Oak Ash Mere Hearth Stone Fern Reed Thorn Elder Holt Birch Ridge |

### Regenerating the tables

    python3 tools/docs/readme_tables.py           # rewrite every generated block in this README
    python3 tools/docs/readme_tables.py --check   # exit 1 if a block is out of date
    python3 tools/docs/readme_images.py           # the thumbnail and contact sheets in docs/readme/

`readme_tables.py` is stdlib only and rewrites only the text between each pair of
BEGIN GENERATED and END GENERATED comment markers; everything else is hand-written. It also warns (and `--check` fails) when a
client name array is out of order with its Rust enum, when `agents()` gains a field nobody
documented, or when an asset has no provenance row. The HUD image `docs/readme/hud_seed1.png`
is a shot: `tools/shots/shot.sh OUT.png --opening=steady --ticks=2400 --inspect=1`, resized
to 1200x675.

## 4. Asset definitions

### Rules every sheet follows

From docs/ART_BIBLE.md and docs/ART_SPEC.md:

- 32 px tiles. People in 48x48 cells (style bible v2: adults about 40 px tall), body plans
  other than the biped in their own cells (`swaps.json` `plan_cells`). Animals in cells of
  their own size (the sheet's width over 9 frames, height over 4 rows: the wolf 32x32, the
  deer 32x48, the bear 48x48). Item icons are 32 px (`items32/`), drawn at half size when
  carried; the 16 px `items/` set is the fallback for a kind with no 32 px icon.
- One palette: `godot/assets/palette/ostrog.json`, 92 colours (11 ramps of 8 steps: earth,
  stone, foliage, water, warm, cool, skin, gold, copper, olive, gem; 4 neutrals). `palette_check.py` fails on any opaque
  pixel off it. Exempt: `light/soft.png`, `overlays/edge_blend.png`,
  `overlays/cliff_shadow.png`, `palette/ostrog.png`.
- Light from the upper left; 1 px darkest-neutral outline on figures, furniture and trees.
- Facing rows are always S, W, E, N (the `facing` field 0..3). W is drawn as the mirror of E.
- Every file has a provenance row; all are generated in this repository (`project-original`),
  none sourced, none hand-pixelled.
- Light and colour grading are a full-screen shader (`godot/shaders/grade.gdshader`); the
  grade may leave the palette, sprite sheets may not.

### Terrain

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/terrain/<family>.png`, written by `tools/art/terrain.py`. Each is 8 variants of
32x32 in a row (256x32); the client picks a variant per tile from its position. `water.png`
and `deep_water.png` are 4 animation frames by 2 variant rows (128x64).

The family drawn for a tile comes from its material (`FAMILY` in world_view.gd, from the
material table in section 3), then client-side variety that the simulation does not store:

| Shown as | When |
|---|---|
| wildflower | grass in spring or summer, in noise patches |
| frozen_soil | soil or clay below -1 C |
| wet_soil | soil or clay while it rains |
| mossy | soil or clay within 2 tiles of water, in patches |
| scree | gravel, soil or grass at the foot of a rise |
| ash | living ground that burned in the last ten days |
| cave_floor | soil, grass or clay under the ground surface |
| snow overlay | living ground while it snows in winter (drawn over the family at 82%) |

Living ground (grass, peat, soil, mud, clay, wildflower, mossy, wet soil) is tinted by season.
`deep_water.png` and `packed_earth.png` exist but no client code draws them yet.

### Overlays

`godot/assets/overlays/`, `tools/art/overlays.py`: `cliff_face.png` (3 variants of 32x16:
earth, stone, gravel/ore, drawn under a raised mass), `wall_face_{wood,stone_block,thatch}.png`
(32x8 south faces under built walls), `cliff_shadow.png` and `edge_blend.png` (8 alpha masks
each: N, E, S, W edges then NW, NE, SE, SW corners). The two masks are not drawn by master's
client yet; ground edge blending is in progress on a branch (section 6).

### Structures

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/structures/`, `tools/art/structures.py`. File names are `STRUCT_NAME` in
world_view.gd (the `StructureKind` order). Frames run left to right in 32 px columns:

| File | Frames | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `wall_{wood,stone_block,thatch}.png` | 16 pieces (512x32) | index is the 4-bit neighbour mask N=1 E=2 S=4 W=8; material 14 wood, 15 stone block, 16 thatch |
| `hearth.png` | 3 | cycled at 6 per second while lit |
| `cooking_pot.png` | 2 | cycled while active |
| `furnace.png`, `kiln.png`, `smelter.png` | 2 | states, not animation: frame 0 cold, frame 1 lit, shown while active (only the smelter is ever active today) |
| `lamp.png` | 2 | states, not animation: frame 0 unlit, frame 1 lit, shown while active (a built lamp is always active) |
| `fire.png` | 4 | drawn on every burning tile (tile flag 64), 8 frames per second |
| `door_closed.png`, `door_open.png` | 1 each | open while someone stands in the doorway |
| `stairs_{up,down,both}.png` | 1 each | shapes 5, 6 and 7 (ladders, shape 8, use stairs_both) |
| everything else | 1 | bed, table, chair, stool, chest, shelf, workbench, loom, anvil, barrel, crate, well, fence_post, stockpile, carpenter_bench; `ladder.png` and `window.png` are not drawn yet |

Designation ghosts reuse these sheets at low alpha with a progress bar.

### Plants, with season variants

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/plants/`, `tools/art/plants.py`. `PLANT_KIND` in world_view.gd maps
`PlantKind` to a file stem: tree, shrub, berry_bush, reeds, grain, root, mushrooms, herbs, fern,
vine, root_crops, flowering_plant, wildflowers, stump, fallen_log.

| Kind | Files | How the client picks |
|---|---|---|
| Tree | `{oak,birch,pine}_{0,1,2}.png`, plus `_autumn` and `_winter` | species = variant % 3 (oak, birch, pine); stage 0 sapling 32x32 below growth 0.35, 1 young 32x48 below 0.8, 2 mature 64x64. Oak and birch use `_autumn` in autumn and `_winter` (bare, snowy crowns) in winter; pine uses `_winter` (snow-laden) only while it is snowing. Falls back to the plain sheet if a season file is missing. |
| BerryBush | `berry_bush_bare.png`, `berry_bush_fruit.png`, plus `_autumn` and `_winter` | fruit above growth 0.6 |
| Grain, Root | `grain_{0..3}.png`, `root_{0..3}.png` | stage = growth x 4 |
| Shrub | `shrub.png`, plus `_autumn` and `_winter` | 3 variants in a row |
| Reed | `reed_{0,1,2}.png`, `reeds.png`, plus `_winter` | young stands by stage; a grown stand is `reed_2` or one of the three `reeds.png` clumps by variant |
| Fern | `fern_{0,1,2}.png`, plus `_autumn` and `_winter` | stage by growth (below 0.35, below 0.8, grown) |
| Herbs, Vine, RootCrops, FloweringPlant | `{herbs,vine,root_crops,flowering_plant}_{0,1,2}.png`, plus `_winter` (all four) and `_autumn` (not herbs) | stage by growth; winter sheets are the dormant plant (dry stems, seed heads, leaf stubs, snow on top) |
| Wildflowers | `wildflowers.png` | 3 variants; not drawn in winter |
| Stump, FallenLog | `stump.png`, `log.png` | single image |
| Mushroom | `mushrooms.png` | cave floors only |

Season sheets are optional: the client falls back to the plain sheet when one is missing.

### Items

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/items32/<name>.png` (about 32x32, with material variants such as `log_birch`,
`block_marble`), derived from the reference sheets; `godot/assets/items/<name>.png`, 16x16,
`tools/art/items.py`, the older set and the fallback. Names are `ITEM_NAME` in world_view.gd,
in `ItemKind` order. Drawn on the ground with a soft shadow, and in a person's arms at half size
when carried.

### People

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/people/`, `tools/art/people.py`. Each standard sheet is 48 columns by 4 rows of
48x48 (2304x192; larger plans use their documented cell). Rows are facing S, W, E, N. Columns
(`swaps.json` `layout`), as `entity_layer.gd` plays them for the bridge's poses:

| Columns | Pose | Played |
|---|---|---|
| 0, 1, 2 | idle, breathing | pose 0, cycled slowly |
| 3 | glance | now and then while idle |
| 4 to 9 | walk | pose 1, 10 frames per second |
| 10 to 12 | swing (chop, mine) | pose 2 |
| 13 to 15 | stoop (field, forage, beat out, douse) | pose 2 |
| 16 to 18 | tap (build, craft, cook) | pose 2 |
| 19 | hold | pose 3 standing |
| 20 to 25 | carry | pose 3 walking |
| 26 | sleep (at rest) | pose 4, with 40 and 41 |
| 27 | sit | pose 5 |
| 28 to 30 | strike | pose 6 (fighting) |
| 31 | hit | pose 7 (struck) |
| 32 | fall | pose 8 (just died) |
| 33 | lying | pose 9 (dead) |
| 34 to 36 | talk | pose 10 (a person talking) |
| 37 to 39 | eat | pose 11 (a person eating) |
| 40, 41 | sleep: breathing in, stirring | pose 4, a breath every 3.2 s, a stir now and then, staggered per sleeper |
| 42 to 44 | tend (kneel, dress, check) | pose 2 with work code 8 (a Tend order) |
| 45 to 47 | drink (scoop, lift, drink) | pose 12 (a person drinking) |

Layers, drawn bottom to top: `body_{slim,average,broad}_{adult,child}.png` (skin colours),
`clothing_{tunic,dress,apron,cloak,coat,workshirt,robe,vest}.png` (cloth colours),
`hair_{01..10}.png` (styles crop, bowl, long, ponytail, bun, balding, braids, crest, curly,
fringe), `overlay_bandage_{build}_{age}.png` when injured, `equipment_{pickaxe,axe,basket,hoe,bundle,bucket,sack}.png`
for the work in hand (hidden while carrying an item, which is drawn instead).

Palette swaps (`people/swaps.json`): each layer is painted in source colours and the client
replaces source[i] with target[i] of the chosen set.

| Group | Source steps | Targets |
|---|---|---|
| skin | 4 (shadow, mid, light, highlight) | 6: deep, dark, tan, olive, fair, pale |
| hair | 4 | 8: black, brown, chestnut, red, blonde, grey, white, auburn (elders get grey, over 60 white) |
| cloth | 8 (primary and trim) | 12: undyed, brown, rust, madder, ochre, moss, leaf, teal, sky, indigo, violet, slate |

What a person wears (`view/look.rs`): a coat in winter or below 2 C; a cloak on a journey;
otherwise by role (cook and healer apron, builder and miner workshirt, farmer index 6,
forager and hauler tunic or a cloak in autumn); children wear a tunic or dress.

### Animals

[Optional image omitted from this source snapshot.]

`godot/assets/animals/`, `tools/art/animals.py`. 9 columns by 4 rows (S, W, E, N): columns 0
and 1 idle, 2 to 7 walk, 8 asleep. The cell is the sheet's width over 9 and height over 4 (the
wolf 32x32, the deer 32x48, the bear 48x48). `ANIMAL_NAME` maps `Species` in order: person (not
an animal sheet), deer, boar, wolf, hare, fox, crow, bear, duck, goat, hart, bat, crawler,
burrower. `chicken.png` exists with no `Species`. There are no fighting, eating or struck
frames: a fight lunges on the walk cycle and a blow shows the second idle frame (#187); frames
of their own are wanted (#195).

### Light

`godot/assets/light/soft.png`, 128x128 white with a smooth radial alpha falloff
(`tools/art/light.py`). No client script loads it on master.

### Audio

All sound is synthesised by `tools/audio/sfx.py` (with `tools/audio/synth.py`), seeded by
each sound's name, and written as OGG Vorbis with a manifest, `sounds.json`, that lists each
sound's variants, bus and whether it loops. The client builds the buses at start: Master,
Music, Ambience (low-pass when the view is underground), SFX (cave reverb underground), UI. The
generated table below lists every sound.

What plays what on master (`godot/scripts/audio/sound.gd`): day and night ambience by hour,
wind, three rain levels, cave ambience below ground, gusts and distant thunder in rain, a fire
loop near burning tiles and lit hearths, water near lakes, work sounds positioned where someone
is working (chop, mine, hammer, hoe, saw, cook, and douse for carrying water to a fire, from the
`sound` field of `agents()`), `flare` where a fire starts and `douse` where one is put out (from
`drain_sounds()`), `door` when someone steps into a doorway in view, animal calls for animals in
view, `ui_chime` on manual save and load, `ui_click` when a panel, the director or the view
toggles or a person is picked, and `ui_page` when the History or away summary opens. There is no
music on master; the Music slider sets a bus nothing plays on yet (music is in progress on a
branch).

### Generators and provenance

| Family | Generator | Provenance table |
|---|---|---|
| palette | tools/art/palette.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| terrain | tools/art/terrain.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| overlays | tools/art/overlays.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| structures | tools/art/structures.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| plants | tools/art/plants.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| items | tools/art/items.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| light | tools/art/light.py | tools/art/provenance.toml |
| people | tools/art/people.py | tools/art/provenance.toml and tools/art/provenance_people.toml |
| animals | tools/art/animals.py | tools/art/provenance.toml and tools/art/provenance_people.toml |
| audio | tools/audio/sfx.py | tools/audio/provenance_audio.toml |

Rows have `path`, `kind` or `command`, `source` (generated or procedural), `generator` and
`licence`. `provenance_check.py` (run by CI) fails on any file under `godot/assets` without a
row. Policy and counts: ASSETS.md.

## 5. Simulation details for testers

### Time

The tick is the only simulation unit (docs/TIME.md, decision D6). Nothing reads the wall
clock.

| Unit | Ticks | At 1x |
|---|---|---|
| 1 tick | 3 game-seconds | 50 ms (20 ticks per real second) |
| game-minute | 20 | 1 s |
| game-hour | 1,200 | 1 min |
| game-day | 28,800 | 24 min |
| season | 5 days, 144,000 | 2 h |
| year | 4 seasons, 20 days, 576,000 | 8 h |

Seasons in order: Spring, Summer, Autumn, Winter. Speeds in the client: paused, 1x (20 ticks
per second), 3x (60), 10x (200); at most 400 ticks are stepped per frame. The "fast" uncapped
speed in TIME.md is not in the client. Night is 22:00 to 06:00 for the simulation
(`Calendar::is_night`); full daylight on screen is 08:00 to 18:00 with two-hour ramps.
Movement: 4 ticks per tile walking, 6 carrying heavy, 3 running, 8 per level change. Ages and
the calendar use whole game-years of 576,000 ticks; a lifespan is about 60 years.

### The arrival epoch (D18)

`WorldMeta.epoch` is the tick the portal opens: (age of the oldest of the ten + 2) whole
years after the world's tick 0. For seed 1 with no rerolls that is 44 years, so a save made
at 07:10 on day 1 has `tick 25352600` in its manifest (44 x 576,000 + 7 h + 200 ticks). The
three step out at 07:00 on day 1 of spring.

- The calendar people see (HUD, History, away summary, season-change lines) counts from the
  epoch: `World::since_arrival()`, `World::calendar()`. Seasons and days are the same on both
  clocks; only the year differs.
- Pasts are chronicle rows at ticks below the epoch. They are shown in the inspect panel and
  on the cards, never in the feed or to the director.
- Worlds without an arrival (`--fresh`, `--seed`, the CLI) have epoch 0 and are unchanged.

### Map and tiles

The default valley is 128 x 128 tiles with levels z = -16 to 8 (`GenParams::default`);
`--small` is 64 x 64, z = -8 to 6. A tile packs into 32 bits as `material | shape << 16 |
flags << 24` (`SimHost.level_tiles(z)`).

| Shape | Value | | Flag | Bit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Open | 0 | | water (shallow) | 1 |
| Floor | 1 | | deep water | 2 |
| Wall (solid) | 2 | | open to the sky | 4 |
| RampUp | 3 | | door | 8 |
| RampTop | 4 | | designated | 16 |
| StairsUp | 5 | | snow | 32 |
| StairsDown | 6 | | burning | 64 |
| StairsBoth | 7 | | | |
| Ladder | 8 | | | |

### What the bridge sends per agent

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: bridge -->
<!-- Written by tools/docs/readme_tables.py; edit the code, then rerun it. -->

`SimHost.agents()` sends 25 ints per living agent (humans and animals); `AGENT_STRIDE` in world_view.gd is 25.

| index | expression in lib.rs | meaning |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | `a.id.0 as i32` | agent id (the number `--inspect=ID` takes) |
| 1 | `a.pos.x` | tile x now |
| 2 | `a.pos.y` | tile y now |
| 3 | `a.pos.z` | level z now |
| 4 | `rendered_shape(w, a) as i32` | Species index as drawn (0 a person, then animals; ANIMAL_NAME order); a person in the Beast-hunger's shape is sent as a wolf until dawn (C75); pose selection uses this same shape (#485) |
| 5 | `a.facing as i32` | facing 0 S, 1 W, 2 E, 3 N (the sheet row) |
| 6 | `lk.body as i32` | build 0 slim, 1 average, 2 broad (BUILD) |
| 7 | `lk.skin as i32` | skin swap target (species.rs SKIN_NAMES: six human tones, then the species' own) |
| 8 | `lk.hair as i32` | hair style 0..9 (file hair_01..hair_10) |
| 9 | `lk.hair_colour as i32` | hair swap target 0..7 (5 grey and 6 white are used for elders) |
| 10 | `lk.clothing as i32` | outfit 0..7 (OUTFIT order) |
| 11 | `lk.clothing_colour as i32` | cloth swap target 0..11 |
| 12 | `from.x` | tile x moving from (a body: where it lies) |
| 13 | `from.y` | tile y moving from (a body: where it lies) |
| 14 | `step` | progress of the current step, 0..1000 (the client interpolates from -> now; 1000 for a body) |
| 15 | `pose` | pose 0 idle, 1 walking, 2 working, 3 carrying, 4 sleeping, 5 sitting (animal eating), 6 fighting, 7 struck, 8 falling dead, 9 lying dead (view::bodies, #185), 10 talking people, 11 eating people (#485) |
| 16 | `lk.child as i32` | 1 if a child (child body sheet) |
| 17 | `lk.injured as i32` | 1 if injured (bandage overlay) |
| 18 | `lk.elder as i32` | 1 if an elder (sent; the client does not read it, hair colour already shows age) |
| 19 | `lk.equipment as i32` | equipment -1 none, else EQUIPMENT index (hidden while carrying an item) |
| 20 | `sound` | work sound 0 none, 1 chop, 2 mine, 3 hammer, 4 hoe, 5 saw, 6 cook, 7 water (dousing); sound.gd plays each as its sound, 7 as douse |
| 21 | `band` | one of a band of strangers (threat.rs): 0 no, 1 yes, 2 out after food; entity_layer.gd draws them dust-tinted |
| 22 | `a.people.0 as i32` | the person's species in the library (species.rs; SimHost.species_art says how each is drawn) |
| 23 | `from.z` | The level the step began on (#324): a change of level is drawn as a climb or a drop |
| 24 | `emote` | activity emote (#192, WP-08 8.4): 0 none, else an item kind + 1 (the work's own tool icon over the head, entity_layer.gd, at 1.75x zoom or closer) |

<!-- END GENERATED: bridge -->

Other read models in `crates/hf-godot/src/lib.rs`, all flat `PackedInt32Array`s: `plants()`
6 per plant (x, y, z, kind, growth x 100, variant), `structures()` 8 (id, x, y, z, kind,
material, active, progress x 100), `items()` 6 (id, x, y, z, kind, count), `designations()` 7
(id, x, y, z, code, progress, materials complete; codes 0 wall, 1 door, 2 floor, 3 + kind x 100
structure, 4 chop, 5 mine, 6 sow, 7 harvest, 8 haul, 9 cook, 10 dig stairs, 11 hunt, 12 craft,
13 tend, 14 douse, 15 beat out), `carried()` pairs (agent id, item kind), `lights()` 4 (x, y, z,
structure kind), `scorched(z, days)` 3 (x, y, hours since it burned), `explored(cid, z)` pairs of
8x8 cells. Scalars: `sky()` 0 clear, 1 overcast, 2 rain, 3 snow; `season()` 0..3; `hour()`;
`temperature()` in C; `calendar()`; `population()` (living humans). `drain_sounds()` returns the
event sounds since the last call as `[name, x, y, z]` rows (`flare` for a fire started, `douse`
for one put out), the latest 64 at most.

### Saves

A save is a directory ending `.hfsave` (decision D8, format D14):

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `world.bin` | the world, named-field MessagePack, format 2 |
| `chronicle.sqlite` | the chronicle, flushed at the same tick |
| `manifest.json` | `magic` "OSTROG-SAVE", `format_version`, `game_version`, `seed`, `tick`, SHA-256 of both files |

It is written to `NAME.tmp` and renamed over the slot, so an interrupted save leaves the
previous one. A manifest that does not match is rejected; a newer format is refused with "save
format N is newer than this game understands (2); update the game"; format 1 is refused.

Where the client keeps them (Godot's user folder for the project "Ostrog"):

| | Linux | Windows |
|---|---|---|
| saves | `~/.local/share/godot/app_userdata/Ostrog/saves/` | `%APPDATA%\Godot\app_userdata\Ostrog\saves\` |
| files | `auto.0.hfsave`, `auto.1.hfsave`, `auto.2.hfsave` (the autosave ring), `manual.hfsave` (F5) | same |
| settings | `.../app_userdata/Ostrog/settings.cfg` | same folder |

Autosave runs every N game-days (settings, default 1) into the next ring slot. At start the
newest valid slot is loaded. If it then fails to load, the HUD says "autosave could not be read
(...); started a fresh valley"; the old files stay in the folder. With no valid slot a hands-on
launch opens the arrival screen. Check a save with `ostrog verify PATH`.

### The tick pipeline

Each tick runs these systems in this fixed order (`PIPELINE` in
`crates/hf-sim/src/systems/mod.rs`, decision D7):

<!-- BEGIN GENERATED: pipeline -->
<!-- Written by tools/docs/readme_tables.py; edit the code, then rerun it. -->

| # | stage | function | what it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | deity | `crate::deity::step` | what the deity notices, and how it leans (never an event of its own) |
| 2 | spells | `crate::spells::step` | a heat wave, a drought, an ill wind... (#278) |
| 3 | weather | `weather::step` | seasonal temperature and sky |
| 4 | plants | `plants::step` | growth by season |
| 5 | thermal | `crate::thermal::step` | frost in the fields, hardening to the cold (#346) |
| 6 | cave_life | `crate::cave_life::step` | mushrooms spread over dark cave floors (#271) |
| 7 | hazards | `hazards::step` | fire, flood, cold |
| 8 | water_flow | `crate::aquifer::flow` | water let in from the rock moves (#323) |
| 9 | water_quality | `water_quality::step` | rot and the dead foul the water; flow and time clear it (#453 part 2) |
| 10 | building | `crate::building::step` | snow on roofs, and roofs that lose their wall |
| 11 | nav | `nav::step` | keep navigation regions current with the grid |
| 12 | projects | `projects::step` | what people want done, proposed with why |
| 13 | settlement | `settlement::step` | the community planner posts designations (hourly) |
| 14 | rescue | `rescue::step` | a way dug down to someone cut off below (#439) |
| 15 | society | `society::step` | roles, newcomers, cohesion, splits, trade (hourly) |
| 16 | societies | `crate::societies::step` | secret societies, and betrayal (#322) |
| 17 | social | `crate::social::step` | good turns repaid (#295) |
| 18 | crafts | `crafts::step` | crafts as personal knowledge: loss, lessons (C70) |
| 19 | fostering | `crate::fostering::step` | orphans taken in (#341) |
| 20 | ties | `crate::ties::step` | visits across a divided loyalty (#348) |
| 21 | expedition | `expedition::step` | caravans form, leave, travel in day-steps, return (hourly) |
| 22 | journeys | `crate::journeys::step` | away on their own and back, still one of them (#347) |
| 23 | threats | `crate::threat::step` | hungry places beyond the valley, and the bands they send |
| 24 | work_sweep | `work::sweep` | execute work plans; lease sweep; designation effects |
| 25 | perceive | `perceive::step` | local perception into knowledge (striped) |
| 26 | needs | `needs::step` | needs decay by activity |
| 27 | decide | `decide::step` | idle agents score goals from needs × traits × knowledge and commit |
| 28 | act | `act::step` | carry out the committed activity (eat, sleep, forage, talk, loiter) |
| 29 | animals | `animals::step` | wildlife: graze, flee, hunt, breed |
| 30 | livestock | `crate::livestock::step` | the herd grows (#285) |

Determinism (D7): purpose-tagged ChaCha8 random streams, no HashMap iteration in the
simulation (clippy `disallowed_types`), fixed system order, no wall clock. Same seed, same
choices, same ticks give the same digest on every machine.

### Bug, or losing is fun

From docs/history/TEST-SETS.md and docs/design-organic.md. Ostrog is allowed to go badly.

Not bugs (do not file):

- people dying of hunger, cold, fire, infection, fever, a fall, a wolf or old age;
- a settlement shrinking or collapsing;
- nobody fighting a fire because nobody there knows how, or everyone asleep when it starts;
- nobody tending the hurt when nobody knows a poultice or there are none;
- a caravan that never comes back; a missing survivor found dead, or never found;
- strangers turned away under a closed welcome or a thin larder.

Bugs (file them):

- a death or collapse whose cause you cannot see in History or the inspect panel;
- everyone in range rushing a fire regardless of what they know (an order nobody chose);
- a crash, a hang, or a frame that stops updating;
- a person stuck in one activity for hours (`STUCK` lines in `ostrog run`), or a stall in `ostrog soak`;
- ledger drift (`ledger drift [...]` not empty), a digest that differs between two identical runs;
- a save that fails `ostrog verify`, or loads with different people, pasts or beliefs;
- a feed or History line showing `#123` or `community 3` instead of a name, or a line that no
  table in section 3 accounts for;
- `missing_assets [...]` not empty in the client log, `UNKNOWN_FLAG` for a flag this README
  documents, `MISMATCH` in the name-array table;
- anything drawn on the wrong layer (people under trees they stand in front of, shadows over
  people, the HUD under the colour grade);
- clicks, pops at loop seams, or anything painfully loud.

How to report: docs/history/TEST-SETS.md (one GitHub issue per finding, label `test-agent-test`,
commit and tag, machine, exact command, expected, actual, screenshot or log).

## 6. Roadmap

The plan of record is docs/history/PLAN-NEXT.md (not included in this source snapshot): what comes next, in order, with
each checkpoint's status, and where every open issue and backlog epic goes. ROADMAP.md keeps
the phases, their gates and the deferrals. Every merged checkpoint is tagged (`c1` to the
newest, `git tag`); its review is under docs/reviews/ and the reason for every fix is in
[docs/history/FIXLOG.md](history/FIXLOG.md).

As of 2026-10-20: checkpoints C1 to C60 are merged (milestones M1 to M4, MP and MH). Next are
the fixes from the QA round of 2026-10-20 (C61 and C62 the simulation, C63 the client), then
M5 (people and power), M6 (the living world's depth), M7 (presentation, with the visual and
architecture roadmap from issues #189 to #196) and M8 (ship: #16, #18, #19).

Branches are work in progress and are not for testing; test `master` and the newest tag.
